## Minute 0, pass 3

**Input**


**Tool `writeBrain`**

```json
{
  "text": "Strategy brain (v1, written before game 1, no game data yet).\n\nPlacement phase:\n- Prefer junction points with 4 neighbours (5, 11, 14, 20) and midpoints of the middle ring early; they give the most mill lines.\n- Block: if the opponent has two on a line with the third point free, place there unless we can close our own mill first.\n- Avoid creating two-in-a-row with an open third point that the opponent can take with a block that also builds for them.\n\nMovement phase:\n- Aim for a \"running mill\": a piece that can move out of a mill line and back in, closing a mill every other turn. Keep such a piece adjacent to an empty point that the opponent cannot easily fill.\n- Keep pieces in mills when possible (they cannot be removed unless all are in mills).\n- When removing, take opponent pieces that threaten an immediate mill first, then pieces that block our lines or sit in their mobility spots.\n- Watch mobility: a side with no legal move loses. Try to leave the opponent with few free neighbours.\n- Endgame with 3 pieces: flying is allowed; check for double threats.\n\nDraws: 40 turns without a mill after placement, or threefold repetition. If ahead in material, avoid repetition; if behind, repeat to force a draw.\n\nProcess: calculate each move by listing opponent's immediate mill threats (two-in-line with empty third, reachable by a neighbour or by placement) before moving."
}
```

-> Your team's brain is saved as version 1 (244 words).

> Strategy brain (v1, written before game 1, no game data yet).

> Placement phase:
> - Prefer junction points with 4 neighbours (5, 11, 14, 20) and midpoints of the middle ring early; they give the most mill lines.

> - Avoid creating two-in-a-row with an open third point that the opponent can take with a block that also builds for them.
> 
> Movement phase:
> - Aim for a "running mill": a piece that can move out of a mill line and back in, closing a mill every other turn. Keep such a piece adjacent to an empty point that the opponent cannot easily fill.
> - Keep pieces in mills when possible (they cannot be removed unless all are in mills).
> - When removing, take opponent pieces that threaten an immediate mill first, then pieces that block our lines or sit in their mobility spots.
> - Watch mobility: a side with no legal move loses. Try to leave the opponent with few free neighbours.
> - Endgame with 3 pieces: flying is allowed; check for double threats.
> 
> Draws: 40 turns without a mill after placement, or threefold repetition. If ahead in material, avoid repetition; if behind, repeat to force a draw.
> 
> Process: calculate each move by listing opponent's immediate mill threats (two-in-line with empty third, reachable by a neighbour or by placement) before moving.
